U=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}

be subsets of indices,
where
t,m\geq 1
and
t+m\leq n-1
.
Suppose
X(t)>\ldots>X(1)
,
Y(t+m)>\ldots>Y(t+1)
and

R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

Then, if

R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
, we have

R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X(1),X\right)=R\left(Y(1),Y\right)
.

Proof.

To start, according to Lemma
A.1
,
for any
i\in U\cup V
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\geq R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Since it is assumed that
Y(t+m)>\ldots>Y(t+1)
,
then for any
i\in U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+m),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)>\ldots>R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Hence, for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\geq R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)\geq R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Since

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),

then for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

According to Lemma
A.2
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X(1),X\right).

Since for any
i\in U\cup V
,
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X(1),X\right)=R\left(Y(1),Y\right).

This completes our proof.
∎

Lemma B.15
.

Suppose
X,Y\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
, and let

V=\{1,\cdots,t\}
,

U=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}

be subsets of indices,
where
t,m\geq 1
and
t+m\leq n-1
.
Suppose
X(t)>\ldots>X(1)
,
Y(t+m)>\ldots>Y(t+1)
and

R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)
.
Denote

\displaystyle\alpha=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

(58)

Define
a
and
b
as

\displaystyle a=\max\{i\in\{0,\cdots,t-1\}\mid R\left(X(1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha+i\},

and

\displaystyle b=\max\{i\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}\mid R\left(Y(t+1+i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=\alpha+i\},

respectively. Then, if
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
, for any
i\in U\cup V
,
we have

\displaystyle\alpha+i\leq R\left(X(1+i),X\right)\leq\alpha+b+i+1,\quad i\in\{0,\cdots,a\},

(59)

and

\displaystyle\alpha+i\leq R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+a+i+1,\quad i\in\{0,\cdots,b\}.

(60)

Proof.

We only prove inequality (
60
)
is true, since inequality (
59
)
can be proved similarly, or be proved in the same way after switching the labels between

X
and
Y
, and relabeling the relevant components of the data.

According to the definitions of
a
and
b
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha+i,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,\cdots,a\},

(61)

and

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1+i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=\alpha+i,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,\cdots,b\}.

(62)

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.1
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\geq R\left(Y(t+1+i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=\alpha+i,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,\cdots,b\},

which proves the left-hand side of (
60
).

In the following, we prove the right-hand side of (
60
) is true.

To start, let us consider the case when

a=t-1
.
According to Lemma
A.1
, for any
i\in\{0,\cdots,b\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)&\leq t+R\left(Y(t+1+i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).\end{split}

Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:5}}t+\alpha+i=\alpha+i+a+1.

Hence, we have shown that the right-hand side of (
60
)
is true when
a=t-1
.

Now we consider the cases when
a<t-1
.
For any fixed non-negative integer
b\geq 0
, let
P{(k)}
be the statement that

\displaystyle P{(k)}:R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+a+i+1\text{ for any }i\leq k.

We prove
P{(k)}
holds
for any
k\in\{0,\ldots,b\}
by induction
on
k
.

Base Case:
We prove
P{(0)}
holds.
Since
a<t-1
, it follows

a+1<t
and

a+2\leq t
.
According to the definition of
a
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(a+1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha+a,

and

\displaystyle R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\neq\alpha+a+1.

Since
a+2\leq t
and
X(t)>\ldots>X(1)
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)>\alpha+a.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)>\alpha+a+1.

(63)

Since
a+2\leq t
, we have
\{a+2,\cdots,t\}\neq\emptyset
, and
\{a+2,\cdots,t\}\subset V
.
Then, since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
,
for any
i\in U\cup V
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right),\text{ for any }i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}.

According to Lemma
A.1
,
for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(X(i),X\right)\geq R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).\end{split}

Hence, for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,
it follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y(i),Y\right)=R\left(X(i),X\right)\geq R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).\end{split}

Since
X(t)>\ldots>X(1)
, we further have
for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\geq R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

Therefore, for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\geq R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\geq R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)>^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:6}}\alpha+a+1.

According to (
59
),
we have

\displaystyle\alpha+a+1=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)+a+1.

According to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)+a+1\geq R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}}\right).\end{split}

Hence, for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),Y\right)>\alpha+a+1\geq R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}}\right).

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),Y\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}}\right)\leq\alpha+a+1,

which proves
P{(0)}
holds.

Induction Step:
We show the implication

P{(k)}\Rightarrow P{(k+1)}
for any

k\in\{0,\ldots,b-1\}
.

Since
P{(k)}
is true, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+i+a+1,\quad i\in\{0,\cdots,k\}.

Notice that
0\leq k\leq b-1\leq m-2
. We have

\{t+1,\ldots,t+k+1\}\subset\{t+1,\ldots,t+m-1\}\subset U
.
Since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
,
we have for any
i\in\{0,\cdots,k\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(X(t+1+i),X\right)=R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right).

Hence, for any
i\in\{0,\cdots,k\}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha+i+a+1

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X(t+1+i),X\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in[n]\setminus U}I\left(X(l)\leq X(t+1+i)\right)+\sum_{l\in U}I\left(X(l)\leq X(t+1+i)\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X(t+1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)+\sum_{l\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}}I\left(X(l)\leq X(t+1+i)\right).

(64)

Since
Y(t+1)<\cdots<Y(t+m)
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),Y\right)<\cdots<R\left(Y(t+m),Y\right).

Then, since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(t+1),X\right)<\cdots<R\left(X(t+m),X\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow X(t+1)<\cdots<X(t+m)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\sum_{l\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}}I\left(X(l)\leq X(t+1+i)\right)=i+1,\quad i\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}.

Since
k\leq b-1\leq m-2
, for any
i\in\{0,1,\cdots,k\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}}I\left(X(l)\leq X(t+1+i)\right)=i+1.

Thus, for any
i\in\{0,\cdots,k\}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha+i+a+1\geq^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:7}}R\left(X(t+1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)+i+1

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R\left(X(t+1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\leq\alpha+a,

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R\left(X(t+1+i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)<\alpha+a+1<^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:6}}R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

Hence, we have
X(t+1+i)<X(a+2)
for any
i\in\{0,\cdots,k\}
,
which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+k\}}I\left(X(i)\leq X(a+2)\right)=k+1.

Since
U=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}
and
k<m
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+k\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)\\
&=\sum_{i\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+k\}}I\left(X(i)\leq X(a+2)\right)+\sum_{i\in[n]\setminus U}I\left(X(i)\leq X(a+2)\right)\\
&=k+1+R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\\
&>^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:6}}\alpha+a+2+k.\end{split}

(65)

Since
X(1)<\cdots<X(t)
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(1),X\right)<\cdots<R\left(X(t),X\right).

Then, since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(1),Y\right)<\cdots<R\left(Y(t),Y\right)\Rightarrow Y(1)<\cdots Y(t).

Then, for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),Y\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y(a+2),Y\right).

Since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\geq R\left(Y(a+2),Y\right)=R\left(X(a+2),X\right).

According to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(a+2),X\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(X(a+2),(X(l))_{l\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+k\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)

\displaystyle>^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:8}}\alpha+a+2+k

\displaystyle=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:3:eqn:5}}R\left(Y(t+k+2),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)+a+1.

Applying Lemma
A.1
again, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+k+2),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)+a+1\geq R\left(Y(t+k+2),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,n\}}\right).

Hence, for any
i\in\{a+2,\cdots,t\}
, it follows ithat

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\geq R\left(Y(a+2),Y\right)>R\left(Y(t+k+2),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,n\}}\right).

Thus, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y(t+k+2),Y\right)&=R\left(Y(t+k+2),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,n\}}\right)\\
&\leq\alpha+a+k+2.\end{split}

That is,

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y(t+1+k+1),Y\right)\leq\alpha+k+1+a+1,\end{split}

which proves the right-hand side of (
60
) when
i=k+1
. Hence, we have shown
P{(k+1)}
is true.
Overall, we have shown that the right-hand side of (
60
)
is true. This completes our proof.

∎

Lemma B.16
.

Following Lemma
B.15
, let
\mathbb{S},\mathbb{A}_{1},\mathbb{A}_{2}\subset[n]
be subsets of indices such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}=\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{1}=V\setminus\{1,\cdots,a+1\}=\{1,\cdots,t\}\setminus\{1,\cdots,a+1\},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{2}=U\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\},

respectively. Then, we have
\big(R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
and
\big(R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
are both permutations of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
. Further, for any
i\in\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).

Proof.

According to Lemma
B.15
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha+i\leq R\left(X(1+i),X\right)\leq\alpha+b+i+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,\cdots,a\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(X(1+i),X\right)\leq\alpha+b+a+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,\cdots,a\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(X(i),X\right)\leq\alpha+b+a+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{1,\cdots,a+1\},

(66)

and

\displaystyle\alpha+i\leq R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+a+i+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,b\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(Y(t+1+i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+a+b+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,b\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\leq\alpha+a+b+1,\text{ for any }i\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}.

(67)

Then, according to the definition of
b
, we have

b\leq m-1
. Hence, we have
\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}\subset U
.
Since
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)

for any
i\in U\cup V
, then according to
(
67
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(X(i),X\right)\leq\alpha+a+b+1\text{ for any }i\in\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}.

Combining this with (
66
), then
for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(X(i),X\right)\leq\alpha+a+b+1.

(68)

That is,

\displaystyle\alpha\leq R\left(X(i),X\right)\leq\alpha+a+b+1,\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

Notice that

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}|=|\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}|=a+b+2,

and

\displaystyle\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}=a+b+2.

Since
X\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
is a vector of distinct real values,
\big(R\left(X(i),X\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
a+b+2
distinct integers. Hence,
\big(R\left(X(i),X\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}

is a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
.

Since
Y(t+1)<\cdots<Y(t+m)
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),Y\right)<\ldots<R\left(Y(t+m),Y\right).

Recall that
R\left(X(i),X\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)

for any
i\in U\cup V
. Thus,

\displaystyle R\left(X(t+1),X\right)<\ldots<R\left(X(t+m),X\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle X(t+1)<\ldots<X(t+m).

Since
\mathbb{A}_{2}=U\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}
,
then if
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
, we have for any
i\in\mathbb{A}_{2}
,

\displaystyle X(i)>X(t+b+1)\Rightarrow R\left(X(i),X\right)>R\left(X(t+b+1),X\right)\geq^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:lemma:4:eqn:1}}\alpha.

(69)

Since
\big(R\left(X(i),X\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)\notin\{\alpha,\alpha+1,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\},\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Notice that
\mathbb{A}_{2}=U\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}
,
and
[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}=[n]\setminus\big(\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}\big)=\big(V\setminus\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\big)\cup\big(U\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+b+1\}\big)\cup\big([n]\setminus(V\cup U)\big)
.
Hence,
\mathbb{A}_{2}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)\notin\{\alpha,\alpha+1,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\},\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{A}_{2}.

(70)

According to (
69
),
when
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
, we have

R\left(X(i),X\right)\geq\alpha
for any
i\in\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Combining with (
70
),
we further have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)>\alpha+a+b+1,\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{A}_{2}.

According to (
68
), we then have

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),X\right)>\alpha+a+b+1\geq R\left(X(l),X\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{A}_{2},l\in\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle X(i)>X(l)\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{A}_{2},l\in\mathbb{S}.

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, for any
i\in\mathbb{S}
,

\displaystyle R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X(i),X\right).

Notice that the above equation still holds when
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
.
Then, since
\big(R\left(X(i),X\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
,
\big(R\left(X(i),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is also a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
.

Similarly, we can show that
\big(R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
and

\displaystyle R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y(i),Y\right)\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

Since
\big(R\left(Y_{i},Y\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
,
\big(R\left(Y_{i},(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is also a permutation of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
. Further, since for any
i\in\mathbb{S}\subset U\cup V
, we have
R\left(X_{i},X\right)=R\left(Y_{i},Y\right)
, it follows that for any
i\in\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{i},(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(Y_{i},(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).

This completes our proof.
∎

Lemma B.17
.

Suppose
n\geq 2
is a positive integer.
Let
V=\{1,\ldots,t\},U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\subset[n]

be subsets of
[n]

such that
t,m\geq 1
and
t+m\leq n
.
Consider two integers
a
and
b

such that
t-1\geq a\geq 0
,
and
m-1\geq b\geq 0
, and define

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{1}=V\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{2}=U\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}.

Denote
O=[n]\setminus(U\cup V)=[n]\setminus\{1,\ldots,t+m\}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O,~\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U)\neq\emptyset,~\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},

and

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O,~\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus V)\neq\emptyset,~\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}.

Proof.

We first prove that

[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O
,
Suppose
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+1+b\}=\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O\big)\setminus\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\big(\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+b+2,\ldots,t+m\}\big)\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O.

Since
a\leq t-1
, we have
\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\subset\{1,\ldots,t\}
.
Then,

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\big)\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O,

which proves
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O
when
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
.

However, when
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}=U\Rightarrow t+b+1=t+m\Rightarrow b=m-1.

Hence,
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\big)\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}

\displaystyle=[n]=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},

which proves
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O
when
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
.
Therefore, we have shown
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup\{t+m+1,\ldots,n\}
.

Next, we show
\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Suppose
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+1+b\}=\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}
. Then

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=[n]\setminus\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+1+b\}\cup O.

Since
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+1+b\}
,
we have
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
However, suppose
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
. Then we have

[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=[n]
and
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Therefore, we have shown
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Meanwhile, according to the definition of
\mathbb{A}_{2}
,
we have
\mathbb{A}_{2}\subset U
, which gives
[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Hence, we have shown
[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.

Next, we show
\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U)\neq\emptyset
. Since

U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
,
we have

[n]\setminus U=(U\cup V\cup O)\setminus U=V\cup O=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup O
.
Notice that
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
, where

t-1\geq a\geq 0
,
and
m-1\geq b\geq 0
.
Since

\{1,\ldots,t\}\cap\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\neq\emptyset
,
we have

\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U)\neq\emptyset
.

Next, we show
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Suppose
\mathbb{A}_{2}\neq\emptyset
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+1+b\}=\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O\big)\setminus\{t+2+b,\ldots,t+m\}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\big(\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+b+2,\ldots,t+m\}\big)\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O.

Since
t-1\geq a\geq 1
, we have

\{1,\ldots,t\}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{1,\ldots,a+1\}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}\cup(\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup O)=\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U).

However, when
\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}=U\Rightarrow t+b+1=t+m\Rightarrow b=m-1.

Then, we have
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup([n]\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\})

\displaystyle=[n]=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}.

Therefore, we have shown
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.

Now, we show
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O
.
Suppose
\mathbb{A}_{1}\neq\emptyset
. Then, we have
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=\{a+2,\ldots,t\}
.
Subsequently, it follows that

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O\big)\setminus\{a+2,\ldots,t\}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{a+2,\ldots,t\}\big)\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O.

Since
b\leq m-1
,
we have
\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\subset\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup(\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\})\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O,

which proves
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup\{t+m+1,\ldots,n\}
when
\mathbb{A}_{1}\neq\emptyset
.

However, when
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=V\Rightarrow a+1=t\Rightarrow a=t-1.

Hence,
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\big(\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\big)\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O

\displaystyle=[n]=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1},

which proves
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup\{t+m+1,\ldots,n\}
when
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\emptyset
.
Therefore, we have shown
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup\{t+m+1,\ldots,n\}
.

Next, we show
\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
. Suppose
\mathbb{A}_{1}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=\{a+2,\ldots,t\}
. Then

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=[n]\setminus\{a+2,\ldots,t\}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}.

Since
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
,
we have
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.
However, suppose
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\emptyset
. Then, we have

[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=[n]
and
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.
Therefore, we have shown
\mathbb{S}\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.

Then, according to the definition of
\mathbb{A}_{1}
,
we have
\mathbb{A}_{1}\subset V
, which gives
[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.
Hence, we have shown
[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.

Next, we show
\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus V)\neq\emptyset
.
Since

V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
,
we have

[n]\setminus V=\{t+1,\ldots,n\}
.
Notice that
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
, where

t-1\geq a\geq 0
,
and
m-1\geq b\geq 0
.
Then, since

\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cap\{t+1,\ldots,n\}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\neq\emptyset
.
We have

\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus V)\neq\emptyset
.

Finally, we show

\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
. When
\mathbb{A}_{1}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=\{a+2,\ldots,t\}
. Then

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O\big)\setminus\{a+2,\ldots,t\}

\displaystyle=\big(\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{a+2,\ldots,t\}\big)\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O.

Since
b\leq m-1
,
we have
\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\subset\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, which
gives
\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\cup O

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V).

Hence, we have shown
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}

when
\mathbb{A}_{1}\neq\emptyset
.
However, suppose
\mathbb{A}_{1}=\emptyset
. We have

\displaystyle\{1,\ldots,a+1\}=V\Rightarrow a+1=t\Rightarrow a=t-1.

Then, we have
\mathbb{S}=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)

\displaystyle=\{1,\ldots,t\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,t+b+1\}\cup([n]\setminus\{1,\ldots,t\})

\displaystyle=[n]=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}.

Therefore, we have shown
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.
This completes our proof.
∎

The following proposition is crucial for proving Proposition 2.6.

Proposition B.18
.

Suppose
X_{1},Y_{1},X_{2},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
,
and
U,V\subset[n]
are disjoint subsets of
indices such that
U\cap V=\emptyset
. Then, if

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U,

(71)

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus V,

(72)

and

R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),

\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
,
we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.

Proof.

For any given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
P{(k)}
be the statement of Proposition
B.18
when
|U|+|V|=k
.
We prove
P{(k)}
holds for any
k\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
by
induction on
k
.

Base Case:
we prove
P{(0)}
is true.
Since
|U|=|V|=0
, we have

U=V=\emptyset
. Then, according to
(
71
) and
(
72
),
we have

R\left(X_{1},X_{1}\right)=R\left(X_{2},X_{2}\right)
and
R\left(Y_{1},Y_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{2},Y_{2}\right)
, respectively.
Hence, we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.
This proves
P{(0)}
is true.

Induction Step:
We show the implication

\displaystyle P{(i)},\text{ for any }i\leq k\Rightarrow P{(k+1)}

for any

k\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
. Assume the induction
hypothesis: when
|U|+|V|=i\leq k
, the cases

P{(i)}
is true.

Suppose
|U|+|V|=k+1
. Then we have either

\displaystyle\text{case }(\mathrm{I}):|U|=k+1,|V|=0\text{ or }|U|=0,|V|=k+1,

or

\displaystyle\text{case }(\mathrm{II}):|U|>0,|V|>0,|U|+|V|=k+1,

is true.

Suppose the case
(\mathrm{I})
:
|U|=k+1,|V|=0

or
|U|=0,|V|=k+1
holds.
Without loss of generality, let us
assume

|U|=k+1
and
|V|=0
.
The other case then can be proved
in the same way after switching the labels of
X
and
Y
.

Let us assume (after relabeling)

U=\{1,\cdots,m\}
, and
Y_{1}(1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(m)
.
According to (
72
),
the order of
Y_{1}(1),\ldots,Y_{1}(m)

equals to the order of
Y_{2}(1),\ldots,Y_{2}(m)
.
Hence, we have

Y_{2}(1)<\cdots<Y_{2}(m)
.
Thus, for both
j=1,2
, we have

Y_{j}(1),\ldots,Y_{j}(m)
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{j}(1),Y_{j}\right)<\cdots<R\left(Y_{j}(m),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for both }j=1,2.

Since
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
,
we further have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),X_{j}\right)<\cdots<R\left(X_{j}(m),X_{j}\right)\Rightarrow X_{j}(1)<\cdots<X_{j}(m).

Then, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)=R\left(X_{j}(1),X_{j}\right),j\in\{1,2\}.

Since
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)=R\left(X_{j}(1),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(1),Y_{j}\right),j\in\{1,2\}.

Next, since
V=\emptyset
, and
Y_{1}(i)=Y_{2}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus V,

then we have

Y_{1}=Y_{2}
. Hence,

R\left(Y_{1}(1),Y_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)
.
Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right).

According to (
71
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U.

Subsequently, according to
Lemma
B.11
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U.\end{split}

Therefore, we have shown that
for any
i\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U)}\right).

Notice that
V=\emptyset
. Hence, we have
|V|+|[n]\setminus(\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus U))|=0+|U\setminus\{1\}|=m-1=k
.
Meanwhile, notice that according to (
72
), we have

R\left(Y_{1},Y_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.
Also, we have
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\{1\},j\in\{1,2\}
. Then, since
P{(k)}
is true, we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.
This proves
P{(k+1)}
when case
(\mathrm{I})
is true.

Suppose the case
(\mathrm{II})
:
|U|>0,|V|>0,|U|+|V|=k+1
is true.
Since
|U|>0,|V|>0
, we have
U\neq\emptyset
,
and
V\neq\emptyset
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume (after relabeling)

V=\{1,\cdots,t\}
,
U=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}
,

X_{1}(1)<\cdots<X_{1}(t)
,
and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\cdots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.
According to (
71
),
and (
72
),
the order of

X_{1}(1),\ldots,X_{1}(t)

and the order of

Y_{1}(1),\ldots,Y_{1}(m)

equals to the order of

X_{2}(1),\ldots,X_{2}(t)

and the order of

Y_{2}(1),\ldots,Y_{2}(m)
,
respectively.
Hence, we have

X_{j}(1)<\cdots<X_{j}(t)
,
and
Y_{j}(t+1)<\cdots<Y_{j}(t+m)
for both
j=1,2
.

Since
U=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}
and
V=\{1,\cdots,t\}
, where
t,m\geq 1
, we have

1\in[n]\setminus U
and
t+1\in[n]\setminus V
. Then,
according to (
71
)
and (
72
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),

and

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),

respectively. Let us denote

\displaystyle\alpha=R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),

\displaystyle\beta=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Notice that it is either
\alpha\leq\beta
or
\alpha\geq\beta
.
Let us assume

\alpha\leq\beta
. However, if
\alpha\geq\beta
,
we can switch the labels between

X_{j}
and
Y_{j}

for both
j\in\{1,2\}
,
and relabel the relevant components of the data.

In the following, we are going to consider the
following two cases

\displaystyle\text{case }(\mathrm{i}):\alpha<\beta,

and

\displaystyle\text{case }(\mathrm{ii}):\alpha=\beta,

separately.

Suppose the case
(\mathrm{i})
:
\alpha<\beta
holds. In other words,

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)<R\left(Y_{j}(t+1),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for both }j=1,2.

According to Lemma
B.14
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{j}(1),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(1),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for both }j=1,2.

(73)

Since
X_{j}(1)<\cdots<X_{j}(t)

for both
j=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(1),X_{j}\right)<\cdots<R\left(X_{j}(t),X_{j}\right),\text{ for both }j=1,2.

Then, since
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{j}(1),Y_{j}\right)<\cdots<R\left(Y_{j}(t),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for both }j=1,2

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle Y_{j}(1)<Y_{j}(2)<\cdots<Y_{j}(t)\text{ for both }j=1,2.

Notice that
V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
.
Next, according to Lemma
A.2
,
for both
j=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{j}(1),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(1),Y_{j}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:eqn:0}}R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

According to (
71
),
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

Then, it follows

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

According to (
72
), for any
i\in[n]\setminus V
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Subsequently, according to Lemma
B.11
, for any
i\in[n]\setminus V
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Therefore, we have shown that
for any
i\in([n]\setminus V)\cup\{1\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Notice that
|U|+|V\setminus\{1\}|=|U|+|V|-1=k
. Meanwhile, notice that according to (
71
), we have

R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U
.
Also, we have
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\{1\},j\in\{1,2\}
. Then, since
P{(k)}
is true, we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.
This proves
P{(k+1)}
when the case
(\mathrm{i})
holds.

Now, suppose the case
(\mathrm{ii})
:
\alpha=\beta
holds. In other words,

\displaystyle\alpha=\beta=R\left(X_{j}(1),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(t+1),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }j\in\{1,2\}.

Define

\displaystyle a=\max\{i\in\{0,\cdots,t-1\}~|~R\left(X_{1}(1+i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha+i\},

and

\displaystyle b=\max\{i\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}~|~R\left(Y_{1}(t+1+i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=\alpha+i\}.

Next, define

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}=\{1,\cdots,a+1\}\cup\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{1}=V\setminus\{1,\cdots,a+1\}=\{1,\cdots,t\}\setminus\{1,\cdots,a+1\},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{2}=U\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}=\{t+1,\cdots,t+m\}\setminus\{t+1,\cdots,t+1+b\}.

Then, according to Lemma
B.16
,
for both
j=1,2
, we have,

\big(R\left(X_{j}(i),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
, and
\big(R\left(Y_{j}(i),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}

are permutations of
\{\alpha,\cdots,\alpha+a+b+1\}
, and

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(i),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

Thus,
\big(R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\big(R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
.
Then, there exist a permutation

(\sigma(l))_{l\in\mathbb{S}}

of
\mathbb{S}
such that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(\sigma(i)),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

(74)

Since
R\left(X_{j}(i),(X_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),(Y_{j}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right),\text{for any }i\in\mathbb{S},j\in\{1,2\}
, then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)&=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\\
&=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:eqn:4}}R\left(X_{1}(\sigma(i)),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\\
&=R\left(Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).\end{split}

(75)

Next, define
O=[n]\setminus(U\cup V)
.
Then, according to Lemma
B.17
, we have

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O,

(76)

\displaystyle[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O,

(77)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U)\neq\emptyset,~\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},

(78)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus V)\neq\emptyset,~\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}.

(79)

According to (
71
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U).

(80)

Notice that
\big(R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
is a permutation of
\big(R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)\big)_{i\in\mathbb{S}}
, and according to
(
71
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)\text{ for any }i\in([n]\setminus U)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

According to (
78
), we have
\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus U)\neq\emptyset
,
and
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
.
Then, we can apply Lemma
B.13

and get

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in([n]\setminus U)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

(81)

According to (
75
),
R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)

is a permutation of

R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)
.
Meanwhile, according to (
72
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in([n]\setminus V)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

According to (
79
), we have
\mathbb{S},[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1},~\mathbb{S}\cap([n]\setminus V)\neq\emptyset
,
and
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
. Subsequently, according to Lemma
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in([n]\setminus V)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

(82)

Next, consider vectors
X_{3},Y_{3}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S},\text{ and }X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S};

\displaystyle Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S},\text{ and }Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S},

where
(\sigma(l))_{l\in\mathbb{S}}

such that (
74
) is
a permutation of
\mathbb{S}
.
Since
X_{3}
and
Y_{3}
are permutations of

X_{1}
and
Y_{1}
according to indices,
we have

\displaystyle D(X_{3},Y_{3})=D(X_{1},Y_{1}).

(83)

Meanwhile, since
X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
, and
X_{3}
is a permutation of
X_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Similarly, since
Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
, and
Y_{3}
is a permutation of
Y_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Then, since
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)
for any

i\in U\cup V
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Since we also have
R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)
for any
i\in U\cup V
, then if follows that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in(U\cup V)\setminus\mathbb{S},j\in\{2,3\}.

Notice that according to the definition of
\mathbb{S},\mathbb{A}_{1}
and
\mathbb{A}_{2}
,
we have
(U\cup V)\setminus\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2},j\in\{2,3\}.

(84)

According to (
76
),
we have
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O
. According to
the definition of
\mathbb{S}
,
\mathbb{A}_{1}
,
O
, we have
\mathbb{S}\cap\mathbb{A}_{1}=\emptyset
,
and
\mathbb{S}\cap O=\emptyset
, which gives
(\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O)\cap\mathbb{S}=\emptyset
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{A}_{1}\cup O\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
.
Since
X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S},\text{ and }X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
, then for any
i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ if }i\in\mathbb{S},

\displaystyle X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ if }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Hence,
(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}

is a permutation of
(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(\sigma(i)),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

According to (
74
), we further have
for any
i\in\mathbb{S}
,

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(\sigma(i)),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right).

Next, since
(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}

is a permutation of
(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}
,
and for any
i\in([n]\setminus U)\setminus\mathbb{S}
,
we have
X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i)
. Then, for any
i\in([n]\setminus U)\setminus\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:eqn:9}}R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right).

Hence, we have for any
i\in([n]\setminus U)\cup\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right).

According to (
78
), we have
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus U)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}
. Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{2}.

(85)

Similarly, according to (
77
), we have
[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}=\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O
. According to
the definition of
\mathbb{S}
,
\mathbb{A}_{2}
,
O
, we have
\mathbb{S}\cap\mathbb{A}_{2}=\emptyset
,
and
\mathbb{S}\cap O=\emptyset
, which gives
(\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O)\cap\mathbb{S}=\emptyset
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{A}_{2}\cup O\subset[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
.
Since
Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S},\text{ and }Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}
, then for any
i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),\text{ if }i\in\mathbb{S},

\displaystyle Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ if }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{S}.

Hence,
(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}

is a permutation of
(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in\mathbb{S}.

According to (
75
), we further have
for any
i\in\mathbb{S}
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(\sigma(i)),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).

Since
(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}

is a permutation of
(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}
,
and for any
i\in([n]\setminus V)\setminus\mathbb{S}
, we have
Y_{3}(i)=Y_{1}(i)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:eqn:10}}R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).

Hence, for any
i\in([n]\setminus V)\cup\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right).

According to (
79
), we have
\mathbb{S}\cup([n]\setminus V)=[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}
.
Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\mathbb{A}_{1}.

(86)

Notice that (
82
), (
85
)
and (
86
) are true, and

|\mathbb{A}_{1}|+|\mathbb{A}_{2}|=(|V|-1-a)+(|U|-1-b)=|V|+|U|-2-a-b=k-1-a-b\leq k-1\leq k
.
Then, since
P{(i)}
is true for any
i\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle D\left(X_{3},Y_{3}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:proposition:equivalence:eqn:5.0}}D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right).

Therefore, we have shown
P{(k+1)}
is true. This completes our proof.
∎

Now, we prove Proposition 2.6 using Proposition
B.18
.

Proposition B.19
.

Suppose
X,Y\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
and
U,V\subset[n]

are disjoint subsets of indices such that
U\cap V=\emptyset
.
Suppose
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
are imputations of
X
for indices
U
,

Y_{1},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
are imputations of
Y
for indices
V
,
and
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),
for any
i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
. Then, we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.

Proof.

Since
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
are imputations of
X
for indices
U
,
then according to the definition of imputations,
we have
X_{1}(i)=X_{2}(i)
for any
i\in[n]\setminus U
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U.

Similarly, since
Y_{1},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
are imputations of
Y
for indices
V
, then we have

Y_{1}(i)=Y_{2}(i)
for any
i\in V
, which
follows

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus V.

Since
R\left(X_{j}(i),X_{j}\right)=R\left(Y_{j}(i),Y_{j}\right),
for any
i\in U\cup V,j\in\{1,2\}
, then according to
Proposition
B.18
,
we have
D\left(X_{1},Y_{1}\right)=D\left(X_{2},Y_{2}\right)
.
This completes our proof.
∎

B.7
Proof of Theorem 2.7

This subsection proves Theorem 2.7. First, we prove some lemmas which
will be useful for proving Theorem 2.7.

Lemma B.20
.

Suppose
X,Y\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
. Let
V=\{1,\ldots,t\},U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\subset[n]

be subsets of indices such that
t,m\geq 1
.
Suppose
X(1)<\ldots<X(t)

and
Y(t+1)<\ldots<Y(t+m)
. Assume
R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)
,
and
Y(1)>Y(t+1)
.
Denote

\displaystyle\alpha=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

(87)

Let
X_{1},Y_{1}
be imputations of
X,Y

for indices
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
, respectively.
Then, if
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in U\cup(V\setminus\{1\})
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)&=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),Y_{1}\right)\\
&=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha,\end{split}

(88)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)&=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),X_{1}\right)\\
&=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=\alpha.\end{split}

(89)

Additionally, we have

X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
, and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.

Proof.

Since
X_{1},Y_{1}
are imputations of
X
, and
Y

for indices
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle X_{1}(i)=X(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U,

(90)

and

\displaystyle Y_{1}(i)=Y(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\}).

(91)

We first show that
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
, and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
. Notice that
\{1,\ldots,t\}\subset[n]\setminus U
,
and
\{t+1,t+m\}\subset[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
.
Then according to (
90
) and (
91
),
we have
X_{1}(i)=X(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
and
Y_{1}(i)=Y(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, respectively.
Then, since
X(1)<\ldots<X(t)

and
Y(t+1)<\ldots<Y(t+m)
,
we have
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
,
and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.

Next, we show that (
88
) is true. To start, according to the definition of rank, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}I\left(Y(l)\leq Y(t+1)\right)

\displaystyle=I\left(Y(1)\leq Y(t+1)\right)+\sum_{l\in[n]\setminus V}I\left(Y(l)\leq Y(t+1)\right).

Since
Y(1)>Y(t+1)
, we have
I\left(Y(1)\leq Y(t+1)\right)=0
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in[n]\setminus V}I\left(Y(l)\leq Y(t+1)\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0}}\alpha.

Since
\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)=\{1\}\cup\{t+1,\ldots,n\}=[n]\setminus(\{1,\ldots,t\}\setminus\{1\})=[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
,
we have
(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}=(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}
.
Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha.

(92)

Since
U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, where
t,m\geq 1
,
we have
1\in[n]\setminus U
.
Then, according to (
90
),
we have
X_{1}(1)=X(1)
. Notice that
(
90
) also means that

(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}=(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0}}\alpha.

Similarly, since
V=\{1,2,\ldots,t\}
, where
t\geq 1
,
we have
t+1\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
.
Then, according to (
91
),
we have
Y_{1}(t+1)=Y(t+1)
. Notice that
(
91
) also means that

(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}=(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:1}}\alpha.

Notice that
[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
.
Then, according to (
91
),
we have
Y_{1}(i)=Y(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus V
. Hence, we have
(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}=(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}
.
Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0}}\alpha.

Combining the above results, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)&=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)\\
&=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha.\end{split}

(93)

Next, suppose
V\setminus\{1\}\neq\emptyset
.
Then according to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)\geq R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Since
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in U\cup(V\setminus\{1\})
,
then we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)\geq R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Since
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
, then
for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(X_{1}(i),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

\displaystyle>R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

\displaystyle=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:4}}R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:4}}\alpha.

Hence, for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)>R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right).

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),Y_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:4}}\alpha.

(94)

In particular, the above equations still hold when
V\setminus\{1\}=\emptyset
.
Combining (
93
) and (
94
),
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),Y_{1}\right)=\alpha.

This proves (
88
).

Next, we show that (
89
) is true.
Suppose
U\setminus\{t+1\}\neq\emptyset
.
Since
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in U\cup(V\setminus\{1\})
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}.

According to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}.

Further, since
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)

\displaystyle>R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)>R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0}}\alpha,\text{ for any }i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}.

Next, notice that
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in U\cup(V\setminus\{1\})
,
and
t+1\in U
. Then we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(t+1),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),Y_{1}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:5}}\alpha.

Then, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(t+1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),X_{1}\right)=\alpha.

(95)

In particular, the above equations still hold when
U\setminus\{t+1\}=\emptyset
.
Combining (
93
) and (
95
),
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),X_{1}\right)=\alpha.

This proves (
89
), and completes our proof.

∎

Lemma B.21
.

Following Lemma
B.20
, let
X_{2}
and
Y_{2}

be imputations of
X_{1}
,
Y_{1}

for indices
U\setminus\{t+1\}
and
V
, respectively, such that

R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(U\setminus\{t+1\})\cup V
.
Then, we have
R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V.

Proof.

According to the definition of imputations, since
X_{2}
and
Y_{2}
are imputations of
X_{1}
,
Y_{1}
or indices
U\setminus\{t+1\}
and
V
, respectively,
we have

\displaystyle X_{2}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\}),

(96)

and

\displaystyle Y_{2}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus V.

(97)

According to the definition of Lemma
B.20
,
we have
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)

and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.
Notice that
\{1,\ldots,t\}\subset[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})
,
and
\{t+1,t+m\}\subset[n]\setminus V
.
Then according to (
96
) and (
97
),
we have
X_{2}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
and
Y_{2}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, respectively.
Then, since
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)

and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
,
we have
X_{2}(1)<\ldots<X_{2}(t)
and
Y_{2}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{2}(t+m)
.

Since
t+1\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})
,
then according to (
96
), we have

X_{2}(t+1)=X_{1}(t+1)
.
Notice that (
96
) also gives

(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}=(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(t+1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0.1}}\alpha.

Next, since
V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
,
we have
t+1\in[n]\setminus V
.
Then, according to (
97
),
we have
Y_{2}(t+1)=Y_{1}(t+1)
.
Notice that (
97
) also gives

(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}=(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0.0}}\alpha.

Combining the above results, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=\alpha.

(98)

Suppose
U\setminus\{t+1\}\neq\emptyset
.
Then, according to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }U\setminus\{t+1\}.

Since
R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)
for any
i\in(U\setminus\{t+1\})\cup V
, we have

R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)

for any
i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}
. Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }U\setminus\{t+1\}.

Since
Y_{2}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{2}(t+m)
,
then for any
i\in U\setminus\{t+1\}=\{t+2,\ldots,t+m\}
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)

\displaystyle>R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)

\displaystyle=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:3}}R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right).

Then, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(t+1),X_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:3}}\alpha.

(99)

In particular, the above equation still holds when
U\setminus\{t+1\}=\emptyset
.

Next, notice that
[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})
. Then, according to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)\geq R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

Since
U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, where
t,m\geq 1
,
we have
1\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})
.
Hence, according to (
96
), we have
X_{2}(1)=X_{1}(1)
.
Meanwhile, notice that
[n]\setminus U\subset[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})
. Then (
96
) also gives that

(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}=(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

\displaystyle=R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)

\displaystyle=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:1:eqn:0.0}}\alpha=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:4}}R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)\geq R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right).

Since
X_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
is a vector of distinct real values,

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)\neq R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)>R\left(X_{2}(t+1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:4}}\alpha.

Since
X_{2}(1)<\ldots<X_{2}(t)
. For any
i\in V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(X_{2}(1),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)>\alpha.

Then, according to Lemma
A.1
,
for any
i\in V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(X_{2}(i),(X_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(U\setminus\{t+1\})}\right)

\displaystyle>\alpha=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:3}}R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Since
R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)
for any
i\in(U\setminus\{t+1\})\cup V
,
we have

R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)

for any
i\in V
. Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)>R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V.

Subsequently, according to
Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:2}}\alpha=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:2:eqn:3}}R\left(X_{2}(t+1),X_{2}\right).

Hence, we obtain
R\left(Y_{2}(t+1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(X_{2}(t+1),X_{2}\right)
.
Since
R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)
for any
i\in(U\setminus\{t+1\})\cup V
,
then we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{2}(i),X_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right),\text{for any }i\in U\cup V.

This completes our proof.
∎

Lemma B.22
.

Suppose
X,Y\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
. Let
V=\{1,\ldots,t\},U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}\subset[n]

be subsets of indices such that
t,m\geq 1
.
Suppose
X(1)<\ldots<X(t)

and
Y(t+1)<\ldots<Y(t+m)
, and assume
R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)
. Let us denote

\displaystyle\alpha=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

(100)

Let
X_{1}
and
Y_{1}
be imputations of
X
and
Y

for
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
such that

R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(V\setminus\{1\})\cup U
.
Then, when
V\setminus\{1\}\neq\emptyset
,
for any
V\setminus\{1\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)\geq R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)=\alpha.

Additionally, we have
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
,
and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.

Proof.

Since
X_{1},Y_{1}
are imputations of
X
, and
Y

for indices
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle X_{1}(i)=X(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U,

(101)

and

\displaystyle Y_{1}(i)=Y(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\}).

(102)

To start, we first show that
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
, and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
. Notice that
\{1,\ldots,t\}\subset[n]\setminus U
,
and
\{t+1,t+m\}\subset[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
.
Then according to (
101
) and (
102
),
we have
X_{1}(i)=X(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
and
Y_{1}(i)=Y(i),\text{ for any }i\in\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, respectively.
Then, since
X(1)<\ldots<X(t)

and
Y(t+1)<\ldots<Y(t+m)
,
we have
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
,
and
Y_{1}(t+1)<\ldots<Y_{1}(t+m)
.

Since
U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, where
t\geq 1
, we have
1\in[n]\setminus U
.
Then according to (
101
), we have

X_{1}(1)=X(1)
.
Notice that (
101
) also gives

(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}=(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:1}}\alpha.

(103)

Similarly, since
V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
, where
t\geq 1
,
we have
t+1\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
. Hence, according to (
102
), we have

Y_{1}(t+1)=Y(t+1)
.
Since
[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})=\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)
,
then (
102
) also gives
that
(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}=(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Since
R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:1}}\alpha.

(104)

Next, notice that according to (
102
),
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

According to Lemma
A.1
,
for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right).

Since
Y_{1}(t+m)>\cdots>Y_{1}(t+1)
, for any
i\in U
,
we further have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{1}(t+1),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)>^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:4}}\alpha.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)>\alpha,\text{ for any }i\in U.

Notice that according to (
103
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha.

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha.

(105)

Suppose
V\setminus\{1\}\neq\emptyset
. Then
for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, according to
Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)-1.

Since
R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(V\setminus\{1\})\cup U
,
we have

R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)

for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
.
Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y_{1}(i),Y_{1}\right)-1=R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)-1,\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Next, notice that
X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
. Hence, for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{1}(i),X_{1}\right)-1>R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)-1=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:5}}R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)-1.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)>R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)-1,\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Then,
\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)\geq R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:5}}R\left(X_{1}(1),X\right)=\alpha.

This completes our proof.
∎

Lemma B.23
.

Following Lemma
B.22
, let
Y_{2}
be
an imputation of
Y_{1}
for the index 1 such that

R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha,\text{ for any }i\in U.

Proof.

To start, since
Y_{2}(1)
is an imputation of
Y_{1}
for the index 1,
we have

\displaystyle Y_{2}(i)=Y_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus\{1\}.

(106)

Suppose
V\setminus\{1\}\neq\emptyset
.
Then for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
,
according to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right).

According to (
106
), we have

Y_{2}(i)=Y_{1}(i)
for any

i\in V\setminus\{1\}
, and

(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}=(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

According to Lemma
B.22
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus\{1\}}\right)\geq R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Since
R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)
,
we then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.\end{split}

Since
Y_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
is a vector of distinct real values, we have for any
i\in V\setminus\{1\}
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\neq R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right).

Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)>R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)\Rightarrow Y_{2}(i)>Y_{2}(1),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right).

In particular, the above equation still holds when
V\setminus\{1\}=\emptyset
. Further, notice that
R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)
,
and according to Lemma
B.22
,

R\left(X_{1}(1),X_{1}\right)=\alpha
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha.

(107)

Next, according to Lemma
A.1
, for any
i\in U
,

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Since
V=\{1,\ldots,t\},U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, where
t,m\geq 1
, it follows
[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus\{1\}
and
U\subset[n]\setminus\{1\}
. Thus,
according to (
106
), we have

Y_{2}(i)=Y_{1}(i)
for any

i\in U
, and

(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}=(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

Further, since
[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
and
U\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
,
then according to (
102
),
we have
Y_{1}(i)=Y(i)
for any

i\in U
, and

(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}=(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{1}(i),(Y_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)=R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

Further, since
Y(t+1)<\ldots<Y(t+m)
, for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq R\left(Y(i),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)\geq R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right).

Notice that we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(X(1),(X(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:1}}\alpha.

Then, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)\geq\alpha+1\Rightarrow R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)-1\geq\alpha.

According to Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)\geq R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)-1.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y(t+1),(Y(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus V}\right)\geq\alpha.

Then, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\geq\alpha,\text{ for any }i\in U

According to (
107
), we have
R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=\alpha
. Since
Y_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
is a vector of distinct real values, then for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)\neq R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)=\alpha

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R\left(Y_{2}(i),Y_{2}\right)>R\left(Y_{2}(1),Y_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow Y_{2}(i)>Y_{2}(1)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:4:eqn:2}}\alpha.

This completes our proof.
∎

Lemma B.24
.

Following Lemma
B.22
and Lemma
B.23
,
let
X_{3}
and
Y_{3}
be imputations of
X_{1},Y_{2}

for
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
, respectively, such that

R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(V\setminus\{1\})\cup U
. Then, we have
R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V
.

Proof.

To start, since
X_{3}
and
Y_{3}
are imputations of
X_{1},Y_{2}

for
U
and
V\setminus\{1\}
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle X_{3}(i)=X_{1}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus U,

(108)

\displaystyle Y_{3}(i)=Y_{2}(i),\text{ for any }i\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\}).

(109)

Recall that
U=\{t+1,\ldots,t+m\}
, where
t,m\geq 1
.
Hence,
1\in[n]\setminus U
. Then according to (
108
),
we have
X_{3}(1)=X_{1}(1)
. Notice that (
108
) also
gives that
(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}=(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(X_{1}(1),(X_{1}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:3:eqn:1}}\alpha.

Similarly, since
1\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
, according to
(
109
), we have
Y_{3}(1)=Y_{2}(1)
.
Notice that
[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\}=\{1\})\cup([n]\setminus V)
. Then (
109
)
also gives that
(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}=(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

(110)

According to Lemma
B.23
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(1),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=\alpha.

Hence

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=\alpha.

Combining the above results, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=\alpha.

(111)

Suppose
V\setminus\{1\}\neq\emptyset
, then since
R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(V\setminus\{1\})\cup U
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)=R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

According to Lemma
B.22
, we have

X_{1}(1)<\ldots<X_{1}(t)
. Notice that
from (
108
),
we have
X_{1}(i)=X_{3}(i)
for any
i\in V=\{1,\ldots,t\}
.
Hence, we further have

X_{3}(1)<\ldots<X_{3}(t)
. Subsequently,

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)>R\left(X_{3}(1),X_{3}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

According to Lemma
A.1
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(1),X_{3}\right)\geq R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:5:eqn:4}}R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)>R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right),\text{ for any }i\in V\setminus\{1\}.

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(1),Y_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(1),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})}\right)=\alpha.

(112)

In particular, the above equations still hold when
V\setminus\{1\}=\emptyset
.

Since
R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)\text{ for any }i\in(V\setminus\{1\})\cup U
, then for any
i\in U
,
we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right).

Then, according to Lemma
A.1
, for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Hence, for any
i\in U
, it follows that

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

Since
U\subset[n]\setminus V\subset[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})
,
then according to (
109
), we have
Y_{3}(i)=Y_{2}(i)

for any
i\in U
.
Notice that
[n]\setminus(V\setminus\{1\})=\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)
. Then (
109
)
also gives that
(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}=(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{3}(i),(Y_{3}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)=R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

Combining the above results, for any
i\in U
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\geq R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right).

According to Lemma
B.23
, we further have

\displaystyle R\left(Y_{2}(i),(Y_{2}(l))_{l\in\{1\}\cup([n]\setminus V)}\right)>\alpha=^{\eqref{supp:theorem:2.7:lemma:5:eqn:4}}R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)>R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U.

Subsequently, according to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(1),X_{3}\right)=R\left(X_{3}(1),(X_{3}(l))_{l\in[n]\setminus U}\right)=\alpha=R\left(Y_{3}(1),Y_{3}\right).

Combining this result with (
109
), we have

\displaystyle R\left(X_{3}(i),X_{3}\right)=R\left(Y_{3}(i),Y_{3}\right),\text{ for any }i\in U\cup V.

This completes our proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem 2.7.

Theorem B.25
.

Suppose
X,Y\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
